=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
Given an integer array nums, find the number of subsequences of size 5 of nums with a unique middle mode.
Since the answer may be very large, return it modulo 10^9 + 7.
A mode of a sequence of numbers is defined as the element that appears the maximum number of times in the sequence.
A sequence of numbers contains a unique mode if it has only one mode.
A sequence of numbers seq of size 5 contains a unique middle mode if the middle element (seq[2]) is a unique mode.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [1,1,1,1,1,1]
Output: 6
Explanation:
[1, 1, 1, 1, 1] is the only subsequence of size 5 that can be formed, and it has a unique middle mode of 1. This subsequence can be formed in 6 different ways, so the output is 6. 

Example 2:

Input: nums = [1,2,2,3,3,4]
Output: 4
Explanation:
[1, 2, 2, 3, 4] and [1, 2, 3, 3, 4] each have a unique middle mode because the number at index 2 has the greatest frequency in the subsequence. [1, 2, 2, 3, 3] does not have a unique middle mode because 2 and 3 appear twice.

Example 3:

Input: nums = [0,1,2,3,4,5,6,7,8]
Output: 0
Explanation:
There is no subsequence of length 5 with a unique middle mode.

 
Constraints:

5 <= nums.length <= 1000
-10^9 <= nums[i] <= 10^9

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def subsequencesWithMiddleMode(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's think about coordinate compression and prefix counts. Need import List. Code:

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def subsequencesWithMiddleMode(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        n = len(nums)
        vals = sorted(set(nums))
        comp = {v:i for i,v in enumerate(vals)}
        arr = [comp[v] for v in nums]
        m = len(vals)
        pref = [[0]*m]
        for v in arr:
            row = pref[-1].copy()
            row[v] += 1
            pref.append(row)
        total = pref[-1]

        def c2(x):
            return x*(x-1)//2 if x >= 2 else 0

        def C(n,k):
            if k < 0 or k > n:
                return 0
            if k == 0:
                return 1
            if k == 1:
                return n
            # k == 2
            return n*(n-1)//2

        ans = 0
        for k in range(n):
            x = arr[k]
            L = k
            R = n - 1 - k
            left_x = pref[k][x]
            right_x = total[x] - pref[k+1][x]
            non_left = L - left_x
            non_right = R - right_x

            # Count A: at least two x among four side positions
            A = 0
            for lx in range(3):
                for rx in range(3):
                    if lx + rx >= 2:
                        A += C(left_x, lx) * C(right_x, rx) * C(non_left, 2-lx) * C(non_right, 2-rx)

            # T distinct pairs among non-x positions
            T_L = c2(non_left)
            T_R = c2(non_right)
            for v in range(m):
                if v == x:
                    continue
                fL = pref[k][v]
                fR = total[v] - pref[k+1][v]
                T_L -= c2(fL)
                T_R -= c2(fR)

            sum1 = 0
            sum2 = 0
            for v in range(m):
                if v == x:
                    continue
                fL = pref[k][v]
                fR = total[v] - pref[k+1][v]
                if fL:
                    sum1 += fL * (T_R - fR * (non_right - fR))
                if fR:
                    sum2 += fR * (T_L - fL * (non_left - fL))

            B = left_x * sum1 + right_x * sum2
            ans += A + B

        return ans % MOD

Let's decide code with A = total - r0 - r1. It is simpler and less prone to C enumeration. Need still use c2 for T. Let's rewrite code accordingly.

Pseudo:

MOD=10**9+7
...
ans=0
for k in range(n):
    x=arr[k]
    L=k; R=n-1-k
    left_x=pref[k][x]
    right_x=total[x]-pref[k+1][x]
    non_left=L-left_x
    non_right=R-right_x

    total_side = c2(L)*c2(R)
    r0 = c2(non_left)*c2(non_right)
    r1 = left_x * non_left * c2(non_right) + c2(non_left) * right_x * non_right
    A = total_side - r0 - r1

    T_L = c2(non_left)
    T_R = c2(non_right)
    for v in range(m):
        if v==x: continue
        fL=pref[k][v]
        fR=total[v]-pref[k+1][v]
        T_L -= c2(fL)
        T_R -= c2(fR)

    sum1=sum2=0
    for v in range(m):
        if v==x: continue
        fL=pref[k][v]
        fR=total[v]-pref[k+1][v]
        if fL:
            sum1 += fL * (T_R - fR * (non_right - fR))
        if fR:
            sum2 += fR * (T_L - fL * (non_left - fL))

    B = left_x * sum1 + right_x * sum2
    ans += A + B

return ans % MOD

Now, let's consider if we can avoid storing full pref by maintaining left counts and right counts dynamically to reduce memory. But pref okay. However, memory with pref as list of lists of Python ints maybe okay. Let's estimate: n+1=1001 rows, each row length m<=1000. Each row list overhead 8KB? Actually list of 1000 references ~8KB. 1001 rows ~8MB. Each row is a separate list object overhead ~56 bytes -> 56KB. The int objects: counts are shared references? Let's examine building pref:
pref = [[0]*m]
for v in arr:
    row = pref[-1].copy()
    row[v] += 1
    pref.append(row)
When row[v] += 1, Python creates a new int object for that entry. Other entries reference the same int objects as previous row. Over n steps, at most n new int objects for updated counts? But wait if the same value count increments multiple times, each increment creates a new int object; previous int object may still be referenced by earlier rows. So total new int objects n (one per element) plus initial zeros. However, row[v] +=1 for a value that was previously updated: the previous row's entry references some int object; after increment, new int object. So yes n new int objects. But there is also the fact that when copying a row, references to int objects are copied. So memory ~8MB references + ~28KB ints + list overhead. Very fine. But wait each row has m references; 1001*1000*8 = 8,008,000 bytes ~8 MB. Good. Time to copy row 1000 times * 1000 = 1e6 refs, fine.

But there is a subtlety: `pref[-1].copy()` creates a new list but entries are references to int objects. If we then `row[v] += 1`, this is item assignment, not mutating int. Good.

Now, if m=1000, loops for each k over range(m) twice -> 2 million iterations, fine. Actually 1000*1000*2 = 2,000,000, yes. Very fast.

Now, let's think if we can compute fL/fR in loops with pref[k] and total - pref[k+1]. For each k, pref[k+1] is row after including nums[k]. For v != x, fR = total[v] - pref[k+1][v]. Potential code:

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def subsequencesWithMiddleMode(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        n = len(nums)

        vals = sorted(set(nums))
        mp = {v: i for i, v in enumerate(vals)}
        arr = [mp[v] for v in nums]
        m = len(vals)

        pref = [[0] * m]
        for v in arr:
            row = pref[-1].copy()
            row[v] += 1
            pref.append(row)

        total = pref[-1]

        def c2(x: int) -> int:
            return x * (x - 1) // 2

        ans = 0

        for k in range(n):
            x = arr[k]
            L = k
            R = n - 1 - k

            left_x = pref[k][x]
            right_x = total[x] - pref[k + 1][x]

            non_left = L - left_x
            non_right = R - right_x

            # A: middle x appears at least 3 times in the subsequence,
            # i.e. at least two of the four side positions are x.
            total_side = c2(L) * c2(R)
            zero_x = c2(non_left) * c2(non_right)
            one_x = left_x * non_left * c2(non_right) + c2(non_left) * right_x * non_right
            A = total_side - zero_x - one_x

            # T_L/T_R: number of pairs of side positions (non-x) with distinct values.
            T_L = c2(non_left)
            T_R = c2(non_right)
            for v in range(m):
                if v == x:
                    continue
                fL = pref[k][v]
                fR = total[v] - pref[k + 1][v]
                T_L -= c2(fL)
                T_R -= c2(fR)

            sum1 = 0  # one x on left, one non-x on left, two non-x on right
            sum2 = 0  # two non-x on left, one x on right, one non-x on right
            for v in range(m):
                if v == x:
                    continue
                fL = pref[k][v]
                fR = total[v] - pref[k + 1][v]

                if fL:
                    sum1 += fL * (T_R - fR * (non_right - fR))
                if fR:
                    sum2 += fR * (T_L - fL * (non_left - fL))

            B = left_x * sum1 + right_x * sum2
            ans += A + B

        return ans % MOD

Need check c2 for x<2 returns 0 because x*(x-1)//2 for x=0 ->0, x=1 ->0. For negative? non_left nonnegative. Good.

Now, let's think about if `one_x` formula for exactly one side x should use `left_x * non_left * c2(non_right)` and `c2(non_left) * right_x * non_right`. Suppose non_right < 2, c2=0. Good. Suppose non_left <2, c2=0. Good. Suppose left_x=0, term zero. Good.

Now, let's think about if A = total_side - zero_x - one_x could count r>=2 but includes cases where total side x count >=2 but middle x appears maybe 3+; yes. But wait total_side counts all choices of two left and two right positions. zero_x counts choices with no x among side positions. one_x counts choices with exactly one x among side positions. So A counts choices with at least two x among side positions. Good.

It does not choose any x on right because right pair is from non_right positions. So right side has 0 x. Left side has one x (multiplied by left_x) and one non-x. Thus exactly one side x. Good.
sum2 chooses two non-x left and one non-x right, multiplied by right_x; left side has 0 x, right side one x. Exactly one side x. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "pre
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def subsequencesWithMiddleMode(self, nums: List[int]) -> int:
        MOD = 10**9 + 7
        n = len(nums)

        # Coordinate compression
        vals = sorted(set(nums))
        mp = {v: i for i, v in enumerate(vals)}
        arr = [mp[v] for v in nums]
        m = len(vals)

        # pref[i][v] = count of value v in nums[:i]
        pref = [[0] * m]
        for v in arr:
            row = pref[-1].copy()
            row[v] += 1
            pref.append(row)

        total = pref[-1]

        def c2(x: int) -> int:
            return x * (x - 1) // 2

        ans = 0

        for k in range(n):
            x = arr[k]

            L = k
            R = n - 1 - k

            left_x = pref[k][x]
            right_x = total[x] - pref[k + 1][x]

            non_left = L - left_x
            non_right = R - right_x

            # Case 1: middle value appears at least 3 times in the subsequence.
            # Equivalent to choosing at least 2 x's among the 4 side positions.
            total_side = c2(L) * c2(R)
            zero_x = c2(non_left) * c2(non_right)
            one_x = (
                left_x * non_left * c2(non_right)
                + c2(non_left) * right_x * non_right
            )
            case_at_least_3 = total_side - zero_x - one_x

            # T_L / T_R: number of pairs of non-x side positions with distinct values.
            T_L = c2(non_left)
            T_R = c2(non_right)

            for v in range(m):
                if v == x:
                    continue
                fL = pref[k][v]
                fR = total[v] - pref[k + 1][v]
                T_L -= c2(fL)
                T_R -= c2(fR)

            # Case 2: middle value appears exactly 2 times,
            # and the other 3 values are all distinct.
            sum1 = 0  # one x on the left
            sum2 = 0  # one x on the right

            for v in range(m):
                if v == x:
                    continue

                fL = pref[k][v]
                fR = total[v] - pref[k + 1][v]

                if fL:
                    valid_right_pairs = T_R - fR * (non_right - fR)
                    sum1 += fL * valid_right_pairs

                if fR:
                    valid_left_pairs = T_L - fL * (non_left - fL)
                    sum2 += fR * valid_left_pairs

            case_exactly_2 = left_x * sum1 + right_x * sum2

            ans += case_at_least_3 + case_exactly_2

        return ans % MOD
```
